I have a trading strategy backtesting and optimization platform with the following components:

- **Backend:**
  - Data processing pipeline with technical indicators.
  - Strategy execution engine with walk-forward optimization and various ML model integrations (XGBoost, LSTM, Random Forest, HMM).
  - Risk management modules.
  - Flask-based REST API layer.
  - Distributed computing and parallelization with Celery and Redis.
  - Structured logging, Prometheus metrics, and Sentry error tracking.
  - Security enhancements including JWT-based auth, rate limiting, and input validation.
  - Comprehensive testing coverage for key functionalities.

- **Frontend:**
  - React + TypeScript + Vite stack.
  - Tailwind CSS for styling.
  - Redux Toolkit for state management.
  - Error boundaries and robust error handling.
  - Protected routes and authentication flows.
  - Integration tests and coverage for core components.
  - Visualization of backtest results and strategy performance metrics.

- **CI/CD:**
  - GitHub Actions CI workflows for automated testing, linting, coverage reports.
  - Staging and production deployment pipelines.
  - Security scanning (Snyk) integrated into CI.
  - Documentation for deployment procedures, incident runbooks, and troubleshooting.
  - Docker Compose for local development environment mirroring production/staging.
  - Scripts for verifying pipeline integrity, local setup, and cleanup.

Please perform a detailed, holistic review of the project as it currently stands. Specifically:

1. **Architecture & Code Quality:**

2. **Testing & Coverage:**

3. **Security & Compliance:**

4. **Performance & Observability:**

5. **CI/CD & DevOps Practices:**

1. **Security Enhancements:**  
   - **API Key Rotation Mechanism:**
     1. Implement a service to rotate API keys at configurable intervals.
     2. Store rotated keys securely (e.g., in AWS Secrets Manager or Vault) and update the application’s environment variables or configuration files accordingly.
     3. Add tests verifying that expired keys are invalidated and that rotating keys does not interrupt service.

   - **Security Headers Middleware:**
     1. Add middleware to include security-related HTTP headers (e.g., `Content-Security-Policy`, `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Frame-Options`).
     2. Create automated tests ensuring these headers are present and correctly configured for both authenticated and unauthenticated endpoints.

   - **Audit Logging for Sensitive Operations:**
     1. Integrate an audit logging system for critical operations (e.g., strategy deletions, user management actions).
     2. Ensure audit logs are persisted securely, and add tests that confirm audit entries are created for these actions.

2. **Observability Improvements:**
   - **Distributed Tracing (OpenTelemetry):**
     1. Integrate OpenTelemetry for tracing requests across backend services, Celery tasks, and external calls.
     2. Implement a trace export to a compatible backend (e.g., Jaeger or Zipkin) and document how to view and analyze traces.
     3. Add basic tests to ensure that traces are generated for known endpoints and tasks.

   - **Custom Strategy Performance Metrics:**
     1. Add custom Prometheus metrics to track strategy-specific KPIs (e.g., average return, Sharpe ratio distributions, execution latency).
     2. Update Grafana dashboards to visualize these new metrics.
     3. Include tests verifying that metric endpoints return expected values under known scenarios (e.g., running a simple strategy).

   - **Automated Alerting:**
     1. Configure Prometheus/Grafana alert rules for critical conditions (e.g., spike in error rate, delayed Celery tasks).
     2. Add a test scenario that induces a known error or latency issue and confirm that an alert is triggered as expected.

3. **Deployment & Infrastructure Automation:**
   - **Automated Rollback Procedures:**
     1. Enhance the deployment pipeline with a rollback step that reverts to the last known good release on failure.
     2. Write end-to-end deployment tests simulating a failed production deploy to confirm that rollback logic is executed properly.

   - **Blue-Green Deployment Strategy:**
     1. Implement blue-green or canary deployments in the CI/CD pipeline.
     2. Add environment variable or feature flag toggles that can switch traffic between old and new versions with minimal downtime.
     3. Test deployments by running smoke tests against both “blue” and “green” environments to verify smooth cutover.

   - **Database Migration Strategy:**
     1. Integrate a database migration tool (e.g., Alembic for Python) into the CI/CD pipeline.
     2. Ensure migrations run automatically on deployment to staging and production.
     3. Add tests that confirm database schema is at the correct version post-deployment.

4. **Documentation and Developer Experience:**
   - **API Documentation (OpenAPI/Swagger):**
     1. Generate OpenAPI specifications for all backend endpoints.
     2. Host interactive API docs accessible at a known URL.
     3. Add tests to verify the docs are up-to-date and reflect actual API behaviors.

   - **Architecture Decision Records (ADRs):**
     1. Document key architectural decisions (e.g., technology choices, microservices vs. monolith, caching strategy) in ADRs.
     2. Provide a simple script/test to ensure ADR files follow a consistent format and are included in CI check for updated decisions.

   - **Improved Local Setup Documentation:**
     1. Update `README` or `CONTRIBUTING.md` with clear steps for setting up the local environment, including running migrations and starting all services.
     2. Include troubleshooting steps for common local issues and ensure all documented commands are verified in automated tests (e.g., via a CI job that runs local setup commands in a clean environment).

Now that observability enhancements have been completed, the next priority is to strengthen deployment automation and infrastructure management. Please proceed with the following tasks:

1. **Automated Rollback Procedures:**
   - **Enhanced Deployment Pipeline:**
     1. Update the production deployment workflow (e.g., `production-deploy.yml`) to include a rollback step triggered upon deployment failures.
     2. Store the last known good release version so that the pipeline can revert to it automatically if health checks fail post-deployment.
   - **Testing Rollbacks:**
     1. Implement end-to-end tests that simulate a failing deployment scenario.
     2. Confirm that the pipeline correctly performs a rollback and logs the actions taken.
     3. Document the rollback process and how to manually initiate it, if needed.

2. **Blue-Green Deployment Strategy:**
   - **Implement Blue-Green or Canary Deployments:**
     1. Modify the staging/production deployment workflows to deploy new releases alongside the current version.
     2. Introduce environment variables or feature flags to direct traffic between the "blue" (old) and "green" (new) deployments.
     3. Run smoke tests or health checks against the “green” environment before cutting over.
   - **Verification & Documentation:**
     1. Write tests ensuring that if the “green” environment fails checks, traffic remains on the “blue” version.
     2. Update documentation to explain how blue-green deployments work, including toggling environments and verifying success.

3. **Database Migration Strategy:**
   - **Automated Database Migrations:**
     1. Integrate a database migration tool (e.g., Alembic for Python) into the CI/CD pipeline.
     2. Ensure that migrations run automatically on staging and production deployments, and roll back if migrations fail.
     3. Add tests that verify the database schema is at the correct version post-deployment and that rollbacks revert to a known stable state.
   - **Documentation:**
     1. Update deployment instructions to include details on how migrations are handled.
     2. Provide a troubleshooting guide for resolving migration issues and rolling back schema changes.

4. **Enhanced Infrastructure as Code & Secrets Management (Optional/If Needed):**
   - **Infrastructure Templates:**
     1. If the project infrastructure is not yet fully codified, consider adding Infrastructure as Code templates (e.g., Terraform or CloudFormation) to manage environments.
     2. Add scripts or pipeline steps to validate and apply IaC changes.
   - **Secrets & Config Management:**
     1. Ensure that secrets and environment variables are managed securely (e.g., using AWS Secrets Manager or HashiCorp Vault).
     2. Add tests or checks to confirm that sensitive values are never logged or exposed.

5. **Documentation & Developer Experience Updates:**
   - **Runbook Enhancements:**
     1. Update the runbook to include instructions for handling rollback scenarios, performing blue-green deployments, and resolving migration issues.
   - **Developer Onboarding:**
     1. Extend `README` or `CONTRIBUTING.md` to guide new developers through the setup of infrastructure, local environment testing of IaC changes, and understanding the deployment workflow.

You have identified several test failures and configuration issues that need to be resolved. Please address them in the following order, ensuring each fix is well-tested and documented:

1. **Database Configuration Fixes:**
   - **PostgreSQL Connection:**  
     1. Add a password to the PostgreSQL connection settings used in the test environment.  
     2. Ensure a dedicated test database is created and dropped before/after tests run.  
     3. Update the `manage_db.py` script and test configurations (e.g., `pytest.ini`, environment variables) to point to the correct test database.  
     4. Re-run database-related tests and ensure migrations apply correctly without errors.

2. **Core Configuration & Modules:**
   - **Missing Models in `core.config`:**  
     1. Update `core.config` to include the `models` attribute or remove the reference if no longer needed.  
     2. Validate that the backend starts and the tests involving the models now pass.
   - **Logger in Cache Module:**  
     1. Import and initialize a logger in the cache module.  
     2. Re-run tests related to caching and confirm no logging-related errors occur.

3. **Redis Connection Issues:**
   - **Local Redis Setup for Tests:**  
     1. Ensure the testing environment spins up a Redis instance (e.g., via Docker Compose or a test fixture).  
     2. Update test configs to point to the Redis instance.  
     3. Re-run Redis-dependent tests to confirm connections succeed.

4. **File System & Audit Logs:**
   - **Audit Log Directory/File:**
     1. Create or verify the existence of the audit logs directory before tests run.  
     2. Adjust the audit logging configuration to handle directories that don’t exist yet (e.g., create them on startup or mock in tests).  
     3. Confirm tests related to audit logs now pass.

5. **Authentication & Token Validation:**
   - **Token Validation Failures & User Auth Issues:**
     1. Review the authentication logic and token validation code.  
     2. Ensure that test tokens are generated correctly and that the `auth_middleware` is functioning as intended.  
     3. Fix user registration/login test errors by ensuring test fixtures accurately represent a valid user scenario (e.g., creating a mock user in the database before testing login flows).

6. **Broker Class & Date Column Handling:**
   - **Broker Initialization & Cash Parameter:**
     1. Fix the cash parameter issue in the Broker class constructor.  
     2. Re-run broker-related tests to ensure that the initial account balance is correctly initialized and tested.
   - **Date Column in Backtest Flow:**
     1. Adjust handling of date columns in the backtest pipeline to ensure consistent formats and proper parsing.  
     2. Confirm that the main backtest tests now pass without errors.

7. **Verification & Coverage Improvements:**
   - After implementing the above fixes, re-run the entire test suite and verify that:  
     1. The majority of tests now pass without errors.  
     2. Coverage meets or exceeds previously stated targets (aim to improve beyond 28%).  
     3. No new issues have been introduced.

8. **Documentation & Cleanup:**
   - Update `README.md` and other documentation files if any instructions have changed (e.g., how to run Redis or set environment variables for the database).  
   - Add or refine test fixtures and mocks as needed, ensuring future contributors can run tests reliably on a fresh environment.

Great! Now I can help you set up the PATH permanently. Here's what you need to do:

Right-click on the Windows Start button
Click on "System"
Click on "Advanced system settings"
Click on "Environment Variables"
Under "System Variables", find and select "Path"
Click "Edit"
Click "New"
Add this path: C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\17\bin
Click "OK" on all windows
Or you can use this PowerShell command to set it permanently (requires admin privileges):

Ran terminal command

> powershell -Command Start-Process powershell -Verb RunAs -ArgumentList '-Command "[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable(\"Path\", [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable(\"Path\", \"Machine\") + \";C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\17\bin\", \"Machine\")"'

For now, you can use psql by specifying its full path: C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\17\bin\psql.exe. After you've set the PATH permanently (either through the UI or by running the PowerShell command with admin privileges), you'll need to restart your terminal/IDE for the changes to take effect.

Based on the comprehensive analysis of the codebase, here's a detailed report of the database setup, issues, and fixes:

Database Technical Report
Database Configuration Overview
Database Type: PostgreSQL
Schema Name: backtest
Required Extensions: uuid-ossp, pg_trgm
Migration Framework: Alembic with SQLAlchemy
Database Structure
The database consists of the following core tables in the backtest schema:

users - User management
strategies - Trading strategy configurations
backtests - Backtest execution records
backtest_results - Results of backtest runs
metrics - Performance metrics
audit_logs - System auditing
Identified Issues and Solutions
1. Test Database Setup Issues
Problem:

Test database setup was failing during the test suite execution
The setup process wasn't properly handling database recreation
Solution Implemented:

Modified manage_db.py to:
Properly drop existing test database connections
Create a fresh test database for each test run
Set up required extensions
Create the backtest schema
Run migrations in the correct order
2. Migration Environment Configuration
Problem:

Schema creation wasn't properly handled in migrations
Version table was being created in the wrong schema
Solution Implemented:

Updated env.py to:
Create schema before running migrations
Configure version_table_schema to use 'backtest'
Add proper schema filtering for migrations
3. Database URL Configuration
Problem:

Hard-coded database URLs in configuration
Inconsistent handling of environment variables
Solution Implemented:

Centralized database URL configuration
Added environment variable support for:
DB_HOST
DB_PORT
DB_USER
DB_PASSWORD
DB_NAME
TEST_DB_NAME
4. Schema Management
Problem:

Inconsistent schema handling across migrations
Objects being created in public schema instead of backtest
Solution Implemented:

Added schema creation in database setup
Modified migration scripts to explicitly specify schema
Added schema validation in verification steps
Current Database State
Schema: Properly created and maintained in 'backtest'
Extensions: Successfully installed (uuid-ossp, pg_trgm)
Tables: All core tables created with proper relationships
Migrations: Initial migration (0bd198de4b14) successfully applied
Remaining Considerations
1. Performance Optimization
Consider adding appropriate indexes for frequently queried columns
Monitor query performance as data grows
2. Data Integrity
Add additional constraints where necessary
Implement proper cascading rules for related tables
3. Backup and Recovery
Implement regular backup procedures
Test recovery processes
4. Future Migrations
Ensure all new migrations explicitly specify the 'backtest' schema
Maintain backward compatibility
Consider adding migration tests
Technical Recommendations

Create a new file pytest.ini in your project root with this content:
Code
CopyInsert
[pytest]
testpaths = tests
python_files = test_*.py
python_classes = Test*
python_functions = test_*
filterwarnings =
    ignore::DeprecationWarning
    ignore::UserWarning
log_cli = true
log_cli_level = INFO
log_cli_format = %(asctime)s [%(levelname)8s] %(message)s (%(filename)s:%(lineno)s)
log_cli_date_format = %Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S
asyncio_mode = auto
env =
    ENV=test
    TESTING=true
    DB_HOST=localhost
    DB_PORT=5432
    DB_USER=postgres
    DB_PASSWORD=456852
    DB_NAME=backtest
    TEST_DB_NAME=backtest_test
Run the tests using:

CopyInsert
# Make sure you're in the project root directory

# Install required packages if not already installed

# Run the tests
pytest tests/ -v

# Create a file test_db_connection.py in your tests directory
import psycopg2
from core.config.settings import settings

def test_db_connection():
    try:
        conn = psycopg2.connect(
            host=settings.DB_HOST,
            port=settings.DB_PORT,
            user=settings.DB_USER,
            password=settings.DB_PASSWORD,
            database="postgres"
        )
        print("Successfully connected to PostgreSQL")
        conn.close()
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error connecting to PostgreSQL: {str(e)}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_db_connection()

"""Basic health check tests."""

import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession

from api.app import app
from api.dependencies import get_db
from database.config import get_async_db_session

@pytest.fixture(scope="function")
async def test_db_session() -> AsyncSession:
    """Create a fresh database session for each test."""
    async with get_async_db_session(testing=True) as session:
        yield session

@pytest.fixture(scope="function")
def test_client(test_db_session: AsyncSession) -> TestClient:
    """Create test client with database session override."""
    def override_get_db():
        return test_db_session

    app.dependency_overrides[get_db] = override_get_db
    with TestClient(app) as client:
        yield client
    app.dependency_overrides.clear()

def test_health_check(test_client: TestClient):
    """Test health check endpoint."""
    response = test_client.get("/health")
    assert response.status_code == 200
    assert response.json() == {"status": "healthy"}
Key Improvements:
Added proper database session fixture with function scope
Added test client fixture that properly overrides database dependencies
Ensured proper cleanup of dependencies after tests
Improved error handling for database connections

### **Objective:**
Improve the test coverage of the project to meet industry standards, address immediate issues, enhance security testing, and ensure the platform is fully prepared for production deployment.

---

### **1. Immediate Issues**

**a. Fix the Database Migration Test Error**
- **Task:** Resolve the test collection error found in `tests/database/test_migrations.py`.
  - **Actions:**
    - Review the error logs to identify the root cause of the test failure.
    - Ensure all necessary dependencies and configurations are correctly set up in the test environment.
    - Correct any syntax or logical errors in the test script.
    - Re-run the tests to confirm that the migration tests pass successfully.

**b. Implement Missing `create_db_engine` Function**
- **Task:** Add the missing `create_db_engine` function in `database/config.py`.
  - **Actions:**
    - Define the `create_db_engine` function to establish a connection to the database using appropriate configurations.
    - Ensure the function handles different environments (development, testing, production) appropriately.
    - Write unit tests for the newly created `create_db_engine` function to verify its functionality.

---

### **2. Test Coverage Improvements**

**a. Add Unit Tests for Core Business Logic**
- **Components Needing Improvement:**
  - `broker.py` (0% coverage)
  - `data_processing.py` (28% coverage)
  - `database/management.py` (0% coverage)
  - `models/` directory (most files <30% coverage)
  - `risk/` directory (most files <35% coverage)
  - `strategies/` directory (most implementations <30% coverage)
- **Task:** Increase unit test coverage for all core components.
  - **Actions:**
    - **Broker Module:**
      - Identify critical functions and classes within `broker.py`.
      - Write comprehensive unit tests covering all possible scenarios and edge cases.
    - **Data Processing Module:**
      - Expand existing tests in `data_processing.py` to cover additional lines and functionalities.
      - Ensure data preprocessing, feature engineering, and data storage processes are thoroughly tested.
    - **Database Management Module:**
      - Develop unit tests for `database/management.py` to cover all database operations and interactions.
    - **Models Directory:**
      - Iterate through each model implementation and create unit tests to validate their behavior and outputs.
    - **Risk Management Directory:**
      - Enhance tests in the `risk/` directory to cover risk analysis and management functionalities.
    - **Strategies Directory:**
      - Write unit tests for each trading strategy implementation to ensure correct logic and execution.

**b. Increase Security Component Test Coverage**
- **Components Needing Improvement:**
  - `api/security/audit.py` (24% coverage)
  - `api/security/middleware.py` (25% coverage)
  - `core/security/auth.py` (35% coverage)
- **Task:** Enhance test coverage for all security-related components.
  - **Actions:**
    - **Audit Module:**
      - Develop tests to verify audit logging, tracking, and reporting functionalities.
    - **Middleware Module:**
      - Write tests to ensure security middleware correctly handles authentication, authorization, and request validation.
    - **Authentication Module:**
      - Expand tests in `core/security/auth.py` to cover all authentication flows, token validations, and error handling.

**c. Add Integration Tests for Critical Workflows**
- **Task:** Implement integration tests to verify the interaction between different modules and components.
  - **Actions:**
    - Identify key workflows (e.g., user authentication, strategy execution, data processing).
    - Develop integration tests that simulate real-world scenarios and validate the seamless operation of interconnected components.
    - Use testing frameworks like pytest with fixtures to manage dependencies and state.

**d. Implement Performance and Load Testing**
- **Task:** Conduct performance and load testing to ensure the platform can handle expected traffic and usage patterns.
  - **Actions:**
    - Use tools like Locust or JMeter to simulate high user loads and identify performance bottlenecks.
    - Analyze response times, throughput, and resource utilization under stress conditions.
    - Optimize code, database queries, and infrastructure based on test results to enhance performance.

---

### **3. Security Enhancements**

**a. Improve Security Middleware Testing**
- **Task:** Strengthen tests for security middleware to ensure robust protection against threats.
  - **Actions:**
    - Write tests to verify that middleware correctly enforces authentication and authorization rules.
    - Test the middleware’s ability to handle invalid or malicious requests gracefully.
    - Ensure proper error messages and status codes are returned for security-related failures.

**b. Add More Audit Logging Tests**
- **Task:** Enhance tests for audit logging to ensure all critical actions are properly recorded.
  - **Actions:**
    - Develop tests to verify that all significant events (e.g., user logins, strategy executions) are logged accurately.
    - Ensure that audit logs contain necessary details like timestamps, user identifiers, and action descriptions.
    - Test the integrity and security of audit logs to prevent tampering.

**c. Implement Security Penetration Tests**
- **Task:** Conduct penetration testing to identify and mitigate security vulnerabilities.
  - **Actions:**
    - Use automated tools and manual testing techniques to probe for vulnerabilities such as SQL injection, XSS, CSRF, and authentication bypass.
    - Address any discovered vulnerabilities by implementing appropriate security measures.
    - Document findings and remediation steps taken to enhance overall security posture.

---

### **4. Infrastructure Improvements**

**a. Add CI/CD Pipeline Tests**
- **Task:** Integrate comprehensive tests into the CI/CD pipeline to ensure code quality and reliability.
  - **Actions:**
    - Configure the CI/CD pipeline (e.g., GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Jenkins) to run all unit, integration, and security tests on each commit and pull request.
    - Ensure that the pipeline enforces code quality standards and prevents deployment of code with failing tests.
    - Automate test reporting and notifications to keep the team informed of test results.

**b. Implement Automated Deployment Tests**
- **Task:** Verify that the deployment process works seamlessly and reliably.
  - **Actions:**
    - Develop tests that simulate the deployment process in staging environments.
    - Ensure that deployments do not introduce regressions or disrupt existing functionalities.
    - Automate rollback mechanisms in case of deployment failures.

**c. Add Monitoring and Alerting Tests**
- **Task:** Ensure that monitoring and alerting systems are correctly configured and operational.
  - **Actions:**
    - Write tests to verify that monitoring tools (e.g., Prometheus, Grafana) are correctly capturing metrics and system health indicators.
    - Test alerting mechanisms to ensure that notifications are triggered appropriately for critical events.
    - Validate that logs are being collected and can be accessed for debugging and analysis.

---

### **5. Production Readiness Checklist**

**a. Comprehensive Test Coverage**
- **Task:** Achieve at least 70-80% test coverage across all modules.
  - **Actions:**
    - Continuously monitor test coverage using tools like coverage.py.
    - Address areas with low coverage by writing additional tests.
    - Refactor code to make it more testable where necessary.

**b. Error Handling**
- **Task:** Ensure robust error handling throughout the application.
  - **Actions:**
    - Write tests to verify that the application gracefully handles various error scenarios.
    - Ensure that meaningful error messages are provided to users without exposing sensitive information.
    - Implement global error handlers to catch and manage unexpected exceptions.

**c. Security Testing**
- **Task:** Conduct thorough security testing to safeguard the application.
  - **Actions:**
    - Regularly perform security audits and vulnerability scans.
    - Update dependencies to patch known vulnerabilities.
    - Implement security best practices, such as input validation, secure authentication, and authorization mechanisms.

**d. Performance Testing**
- **Task:** Validate the application’s performance under expected and peak loads.
  - **Actions:**
    - Conduct regular performance benchmarks.
    - Optimize code and infrastructure based on performance test results.
    - Implement caching and other performance-enhancing techniques as needed.

**e. Integration Testing**
- **Task:** Ensure seamless interaction between all components and services.
  - **Actions:**
    - Maintain a suite of integration tests that cover all critical workflows.
    - Continuously update integration tests to reflect changes in the application architecture.

**f. Database Migration Testing**
- **Task:** Verify that database migrations are executed correctly without data loss or corruption.
  - **Actions:**
    - Write tests to simulate database migrations in a controlled environment.
    - Ensure that migrations are reversible and can be rolled back if necessary.
    - Validate data integrity post-migration.

---

### **6. Documentation and Best Practices**

**a. Update Documentation**
- **Task:** Ensure all aspects of the platform are well-documented.
  - **Actions:**
    - Update test documentation to include new tests and coverage reports.
    - Provide clear guidelines on running tests, interpreting results, and contributing to test suites.
    - Document any changes made to the codebase during the improvement process.

**b. Implement Best Practices**
- **Task:** Adhere to industry best practices to maintain high code quality and reliability.
  - **Actions:**
    - Follow coding standards and linting rules for both frontend and backend (e.g., ESLint for JavaScript, Pylint for Python).
    - Conduct regular code reviews to ensure adherence to best practices.
    - Encourage the use of meaningful commit messages and proper version control workflows.

---

### **Deliverables:**
1. **Resolved Immediate Issues:**
   - Fixed test errors in `tests/database/test_migrations.py`.
   - Implemented the `create_db_engine` function in `database/config.py` with corresponding tests.

2. **Enhanced Test Coverage:**
   - Achieved at least 70-80% test coverage across all core components, API layers, and security modules.
   - Comprehensive unit, integration, and performance tests in place.

3. **Strengthened Security:**
   - Improved security middleware testing.
   - Expanded audit logging tests.
   - Completed security penetration tests with vulnerabilities addressed.

4. **Improved Infrastructure:**
   - Integrated comprehensive tests into the CI/CD pipeline.
   - Automated deployment tests and ensured reliable deployment processes.
   - Configured and tested monitoring and alerting systems.

5. **Production Readiness:**
   - Fully addressed the production readiness checklist with all items marked as ✅.
   - Ensured robust error handling, security, and performance optimizations.

6. **Updated Documentation:**
   - Comprehensive and up-to-date documentation covering test suites, deployment processes, and maintenance guidelines.

7. **Adherence to Best Practices:**
   - Maintained high code quality through best practices, regular code reviews, and continuous integration.

---

### **Timeline:**
- **Phase 1:** Fix Immediate Issues (1 week)
- **Phase 2:** Enhance Unit Test Coverage (2 weeks)
- **Phase 3:** Strengthen Security Testing (2 weeks)
- **Phase 4:** Implement Integration and Performance Tests (2 weeks)

- **Security Focus:** Given the critical nature of security, allocate sufficient time and resources to thoroughly test and secure all components.

### **3. Security Enhancements**

- **Task:** Ensure seamless interaction between all components and services.

**f. Database Migration Testing**
